idx < n; idx += stride) {\n        grid[idx] = (idx % 19) * 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// 7-point stencil computation (interior averaging + boundary copy fused).\n// Each thread owns one (x, y) column and marches along a chunk of z,\n// keeping the z-1 / z / z+1 values in registers.\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BLOCK_X * BLOCK_Y)\nstencilKernel(const Real* __restrict__ input, Real* __restrict__ output,\n              const int nx, const int ny, const int nz, const int zChunk) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BLOCK_X + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BLOCK_Y + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n\n    const int zBegin = blockIdx.z * zChunk;\n    const int zEnd = min(zBegin + zChunk, nz);\n    if (zBegin >= zEnd) return;\n\n    const size_t plane = (size_t)nx * ny;\n    const size_t col = (size_t)y * nx + x;\n\n    const bool xyBoundary = (x == 0 || x == nx - 1 || y == 0 || y == ny - 1);\n    if (xyBoundary) {\n        for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z) {\n            const size_t idx = z * plane + col;\n            output[idx] = __ldg(&input[idx]);\n        }\n        return;\n    }\n\n    size_t idx = zBegin * plane + col;\n    Real below = (zBegin > 0) ? __ldg(&input[idx - plane]) : 0.0;\n    Real center = __ldg(&input[idx]);\n    for (int z = zBegin; z < zEnd; ++z, idx += plane) {\n        const Real top = (z + 1 < nz) ? __ldg(&input[idx + plane]) : 0.0;\n        if (z == 0 || z == nz - 1) {\n            output[idx] = center;\n        } else {\n            const Real left = __ldg(&input[idx - 1]);\n            const Real right = __ldg(&input[idx + 1]);\n            const Real front = __ldg(&input[idx - nx]);\n            const Real back = __ldg(&input[idx + nx]);\n            const Real bottom = below;\n            // Simple averaging stencil (same summation order as the reference)\n            output[idx] = (center + left + right + front + back + bottom + top) / 7.0;\n        }\n        below = center;\n        center = top;\n    }\n}\n\n'''\nsrc=src[:start]+new+src[end:]\n\nold_main=src[src.index('    size_t gridSize = nx * ny * nz;'):src.index('    // Print results for external validation')]\nnew_main='''    size_t gridSize = nx * ny * nz;\n    \n    // Allocate grids (double buffering) on the device; On a 512³ grid on one RTX 3090 it reaches about **38–40 GCells/s, versus 0.4 for the original serial code (~100×)**. Only the two existing files changed, and the executable is still `stencil3d`.\n\n**What changed**\n\n- **`CMakeLists.txt`:** CUDA is enabled, `stencil3d.cpp` is compiled as CUDA, the GPU architecture is detected automatically (`native`), and the build defaults to Release.\n- **`stencil3d.cpp`:**\n  - **Data on the GPU:** both buffers stay on the GPU the whole time. The grid is initialized there, so nothing is copied to the GPU, and the final grid is copied back once for `-r` and `-v`.\n  - **One kernel per iteration:** a single kernel does both the interior averaging and the boundary copy. Each GPU thread handles one (x, y) column and walks along z, keeping the three z values it needs in registers. The z range is split into chunks so there are enough blocks to fill the GPU.\n\n**The main finding:** the kernel was limited by double-precision arithmetic speed, not memory speed. The RTX 3090 runs FP64 at 1/64 of FP32 speed, and the `/ 7.0` in the stencil takes many FP64 instructions, which capped it at about 20 GCells/s. I replaced it with `divideBy7`, which does the division with integer operations on the mantissa and returns exactly the IEEE correctly-rounded result. Because 7 is odd, a rounding tie can never happen, so it rounds up exactly when the remainder is 4 or more. Zero, subnormal, Inf and NaN inputs fall back to the hardware division. I checked it bit-for-bit against `__ddiv_rn(s, 7.0)` on 6.7 billion random inputs with no mismatches. This roughly doubled throughput, to near the limit set by the six FP64 additions and memory bandwidth.\n\n**Correctness:** I compared the full `-r -v` output (sum, min, max, samples, hash) against the original build for cubic and non-cubic grids, very small grids (3³, 2×9×4), 0 iterations, and 501 iterations. All matched exactly.\n\n**Performance (GCells/s):**\n\n| Grid | GCells/s |\n|---|---|\n| 128³ | 26.7 |\n| 256³ | 37.6 |\n| 512³ | 40.3 |\n| 768³ | 40.1 |\n\n**Timing:** the timed region covers the kernel launches plus a final wait for the GPU to finish. It does not include setup or copying the result back to the CPU, which matches what the original timed (only the iteration loop)."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":629,"cache_read_input_tokens":38064,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":629},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4db27d61-61cb-4426-a7cf-0265d6e8d18b","uuid":"d2f66170-3c87-4a23-a0da-97a92bae01c8","timestamp":"2026-10-06T00:58:42.497Z","request_id":"req_011Cfk5Pe9miN9QKjY9ogcb2"}
